Open — product decision for manual reviewFixture 1086697 (Projeto do KinGui vs Yawara, CCT South America 2026 Series #7, 2026-10-25T19:00:00Z) is published from our own originated Valve-VRS line at 51.0% / 49.0%: the required directional pick is Projeto do KinGui on a 1.2 percentage-point edge. The arithmetic is fully reproducible from Valve's official standings file and Valve's published fit slope, and the row is labelled as a non-market estimate, but the model's own published calibration spread is far wider than 1.2 points. The product question is whether such a thin originated edge should be presented as a pick at all, since no market line exists for this fixture and the alternative is an unresolved row rather than a different price. Left visible for manual review instead of being suppressed, rounded, or strengthened.Valve official global standings used as the model input ↗Valve's published expectation formula ↗Valve's published model-fit calibration ↗Hotspawn fixture inventory (locks only, never a probability) ↗

Repaired — ambiguous listing evidence fails closed; live coverage impact unverifiedSynthetic regressions showed that bookmaker-listing parsing could join a name to a different JSON object’s start, miss a valid later widget, or choose the first of conflicting labelled price pairs. The adapter now requires one explicit structured event and a unique label-bound pair across all widgets. Duplicate event declarations, conflicting prices, absent timezones, placeholders and invalid observation clocks cannot renew evidence. The 17 parser regressions are synthetic tests, not real bookmaker prices; no extra live coverage is attributed to this repair without a new source observation.Project adapter: explicit event identity and unique two-sided reading ↗Synthetic ambiguity, source-clock and reobservation regressions ↗

Method limitationThese are our calculated, margin-removed market lines, not a calibrated independent performance model or multi-book consensus. HLTV rankings, historical win rates, Valve points and fan votes are not converted into invented percentages. Team-organization aliases are narrowly sourced; they do not certify the current player roster. Missing information cannot justify a forced pick or a default 50/50. Update 15 October 2026 (session e54807b3): where no market line passes its gates, Valve Regional Standings points are now converted into an originated line, using Valve's own published expectation formula and fit slope rather than a formula of ours; every such row is labelled as our model line. HLTV rankings and fan votes are still never converted into percentages.Official Polymarket documentation ↗Project's sourced identity register ↗VRS model code ↗Valve fit.js ↗

Needs review — policy decisionCoverage decision: a fixture with no other usable line may now be priced from a two-sided exchange quote that fails only the 25-point spread ceiling, provided the market is open, accepting orders, has a fresh observation and update, and holds at least $100 of liquidity. The real spread and the strict rejection are published on the row and in the evidence ledger, the row is labelled low confidence, and a 60-point ceiling still applies. This raises coverage without inventing numbers, but a wide mid is a low-precision estimate and is not comparable in quality to a strict-tier line.Example: mellren vs Privateer Gaming (spread 0.40, liquidity $2,966) ↗Tier tests and gates ↗
Needs review — policy decisionCoverage decision: when no market line exists at all, a pick may be published from an exact HLTV fixture identity with both teams' world rankings, choosing the better-ranked side. No percentage or fair line is published for these rows and every one is labelled research-only, so a ranked comparison is never presented as a market probability. Rows without both verified ranks stay unresolved.HLTV fixtures and rankings ↗research_rank_pick implementation ↗

Needs review — policy decisionFor fixtures with no market line and no two-team ranking pair, a second research tier now publishes a pick with no probability when exactly one team carries a published HLTV world ranking in that exact fixture and the other team has no ranking row there. The row stores both teams' evidence (fixture label, HLTV label, rank, HLTV ranking link, and the primary link for any label mapping) and both validators re-derive the pick from that stored evidence. The tier is deliberately limited: it can never publish a percentage, never applies when the other team's ranking rows conflict, and never fills a blank with a guess. Two rows used it in the 22:32 UTC snapshot.HLTV world ranking row for Procyon (#119), the ranking link stored on the row ↗HLTV world ranking row for INFINITE (#86), the ranking link stored on the row ↗Hotspawn fixture inventory that lists both fixtures ↗

Needs review — policy decisionNew originated-line tier, added at the user's request that every match get a pick or percentage. Inputs are Valve's official Regional Standings points, the formula is Valve's own fit.js expectation (Glicko, rating deviation 75), and the calibration is the slope from Valve's published model-fit chart (0.7986), applied symmetrically. Used only when no market line passes its gates; beside market rows it is shown for comparison and never changes the pick. On the 22:47 UTC capture it differed from market lines by 5.9 percentage points on average and agreed on the favourite in 15 of 16 comparable open matches (the exception: Voca vs Phantom). Valve's repeated labels for older rosters are never guessed: a label is used only when unique, or when the HLTV lineup shares at least three players with exactly one listed roster (Valve's own roster rule). Standings update monthly, so recent roster moves are a known blind spot.Valve fit.js ↗Valve glicko.js ↗Valve model fit ↗Valve roster rule ↗Methodology ↗

Needs review — two official sources disagree by one minuteHotspawn's official rules page says picks lock 5 minutes before the scheduled start. Needs review — evidence-handling irregularity, corrected in this session's own textManual retrieval of Polymarket's Gamma API through this project's page-retrieval path can be served from an HTTP cache. Demonstrated twice on 16 October 2026 (UTC): the URL https://gamma-api.polymarket.com/markets?slug=cs2-lgc-bb3-2026-10-16 was read at two different times and returned byte-identical payloads stamped updatedAt 2026-10-15T22:45:08Z, while a cache-busted read of the same market (?cb=...) returned updatedAt 2026-10-15T23:17:08Z with different prices (0.635 / 0.365 instead of 0.64 / 0.36), a different spread (0.07 instead of 0.10) and different liquidity (8149.023 instead of 6847.5095). Market 5733288 behaved the same way: a cached state stamped 22:51:08Z read 0.69 / 0.31 while the cache-busted state stamped 23:17:08Z read 0.685 / 0.315. Consequences: (1) a manual read must be dated by the updatedAt inside its own payload, never by the moment of the read; (2) an earlier draft of this session's analysis text described the Aurora-9z line as having moved toward the favourite on the strength of a stale payload, and that text has been corrected to the full 0.685 -> 0.69 -> 0.685 sequence; (3) no published probability on this site was affected, because probabilities are produced only by the refresh on an Actions runner, which adjusts observation time for HTTP cache Age, and never from a hand-made read. Both receipts in research/day3-0e407ec7/quotes/ now carry a cache_warning and list every observation separately with its own stamp. Anyone doing hand-made market reads: cache-bust the URL.Cached read of market 5763247 (state stamped 22:45:08Z) ↗Cache-busted read of the same market (state stamped 23:17:08Z) ↗Cache-busted read of market 5733288 (state stamped 23:17:08Z) ↗Receipt with all three dated observations ↗Verification report, rows B4, B8, B14 and irregularity 8 ↗
Needs review — automation limitation, cadence tightened, not eliminatedMeasured on 2026-10-16T04:18Z by session ab18fe2a through the official GitHub Actions API, not inferred. The complete run history for the 'Verify, refresh and publish CS2 Scout' workflow contains exactly two schedule events, both under the old six-hour cron: 2026-10-15T16:59:51Z (success, roughly 4h42m after its 12:17 slot) and 2026-10-15T21:22:25Z (failure, roughly 3h05m after its 18:17 slot). After the two-hour cron merged at 23:20 UTC on 15 October, ZERO scheduled runs fired in the next five hours: nothing at 00:17 and nothing at 02:17. The workflow itself reports state 'active', so this is GitHub scheduling delay, not a disabled workflow. Consequence: the live site served the 00:02 UTC push build while every one of its 20 open picks carried an expiry of 2026-10-16T05:33:49Z, so without a push the entire pick board would have gone stale at once. Mitigation applied in this session: the cron is now hourly ('17 * * * *', twelve slots a day instead of six) so a delayed run still lands inside the eight-hour evidence window. This is a reduction in risk, not a fix: GitHub states it may delay or drop scheduled jobs, and no cron can guarantee delivery. The site never shows stale data as fresh - expiry is enforced in the build and again in the browser - so the failure mode is a visible staleness warning rather than a wrong number. Note also that the GitHub App token available to this project cannot create workflow_dispatch events (HTTP 403), so a push to main is the only trigger this project can reliably drive itself.GitHub Actions run history for this repository ↗GitHub documentation: scheduled events may be delayed or dropped during high load ↗The workflow file, showing the hourly cron ↗

Policy decision — an unpriced fixture now shows its evidence, not just a blankThe user asked for a pick on every listed fixture and, where one cannot be made, for the reason plus the external evidence that was found. The remedy is transparency, not a looser gate: the executive summary now ends with a gap ledger (rendered by gap_ledger in scripts/build.py) that lists every source the collector tried for each unpriced open row, and where a quote exists but failed a gate it is shown with its own numbers - mid price, best bid and ask, the bid-ask gap in points against the 25-point ceiling, liquidity, volume and how many hours before the snapshot the exchange last moved it - labelled 'rejected observation'. Duplicate rejections from the strict and wide passes are listed once. This is also why the eight-hour freshness window was NOT relaxed: only the wide tier could have used the thin quotes below, pick_deadline() would have returned an expiry already in the past, and the independent auditor would have needed a new branch. Two automation additions make the gap measurable instead of anecdotal: scripts/coverage_gap_dedd2e34.py diffs the board against https://www.hltv.org/events/8932/matches on every harvest (it reuses the collector's HTTP client, because HLTV answers 403 to a plain urllib request from the same runner and 200 to the client the pipeline uses), and scripts/harvest_dedd2e34.py replays every published probability from its raw inputs with an explicit mode per source type - outcomePrices are divided by their sum, bookmaker decimal odds are inverted and de-margined; conflating the two reproduced rows only in swapped order and produced a false 'site is swapped' claim.Harvest summary with the ledger and coverage counts ↗The run that produced it (artifacts retained) ↗The full research queue on the live site ↗

Automation limitation — fixed with a measured backstop on top of the hourly cronTwo sessions measured the same failure independently on 16 October 2026 (UTC): GitHub ran the schedule 3h05m and 4h42m late on 15 October, the last scheduled run before this was 2026-10-15T21:22:25Z (a failure), and nothing ran for the next nine hours, so a cron-only design cannot honour an eight-hour evidence window. Session ab18fe2a tightened the cron to hourly at :17 in .github/workflows/pages.yml and set refresh_hours to 1 in the snapshot; that change is adopted here, not duplicated. This session adds the backstop that a cron cannot provide: .github/workflows/refresh-watchdog.yml runs every 30 minutes, reads the published build-info.json receipt and the pages.yml run list, and dispatches the same verified workflow onto the default branch only when the published snapshot is older than 90 minutes - never while a run is in progress, never inside a 45-minute cooldown, at most ten times per 24 hours, and it exits non-zero when the receipt cannot be read rather than silently doing nothing. The trigger is the age of the published board, not the schedule, so a delayed or dropped cron costs freshness instead of silence. Logic lives in scripts/refresh_watchdog.py with 12 unit tests.pages.yml run history (schedule events) ↗Watchdog workflow ↗Session ab18fe2a's cadence diagnosis ↗

Fixed in this change — awaiting scheduled-run proofScheduled publish runs 38902730567 (12:46 UTC) and 38936303441 (17:00 UTC) on 16 October 2026, and the PR #41 push run, all passed verify-build and deployed successfully, then failed the step 'Verify live Pages bytes match this exact build' within about a second. Measured cause (GitHub Actions jobs API + the live build receipt): run 38936303441 built at 17:00:47Z, its deploy step ran 17:01:52-17:01:57Z, and verification fetched build-info.json once at 17:01:57Z while deployment 7124811549 was still updating (until 17:02:00Z), read the previous deployment's older bytes, and failed. The live site then served exactly that run's build (built_at 17:00:47Z, revision 40b13d3, 83 rows, read cache-busted at ~19:30Z): publication had succeeded; only the receipt failed, so every refresh looked broken while the board was in fact updating. The old verifier retried only on HTTP errors and never waited for propagation. Fix: scripts/verify_live.py polls with a fresh cache-busting query for up to six minutes until the live manifest is byte-identical to the run's manifest (or a provably newer byte-consistent build supersedes it), then hashes every file; older/torn/unreadable states still fail closed. Covered by tests/test_verify_live_9a81b26f.py (11 tests). Pages remains in legacy main:/ mode (Pages API: build_type legacy), so push-triggered runs still race GitHub's own pages-build-deployment of the committed root fallback; switching the Pages source to GitHub Actions would remove that race but changes repository settings and was not done from this branch. Proof standard: the next scheduled runs on main must show green deploy jobs with a polling receipt; until then this stays flagged.Scheduled run: verify-build green, deploy-verify failed in <1s (17:01:57Z) ↗Earlier scheduled run with the same failure shape (12:46Z) ↗Live build receipt (cache-bust when checking by hand) ↗

Latent risk found and fixed — no published row is affected; re-verify on the next refreshA refresh can silently lose its picks. In the 2026-10-16T23:53:53Z harvest (run 38983767301, receipts in research/harvest-498cc7dd/) eight open Hotspawn CS2 fixtures came back with no pick at all: 1079170 BET-M vs Just Players, 1081126 Banda chuya vs Enjoy, 1081521 ex-6666 vs Privateer, 1081522 antidaun vs Phantom Academy, 1081527 aimclub vs BASEMENT BOYS, 1081532 ASTRAL vs Leo, 1081861 Lynn Vision vs Morningstar and 1081863 Chinggis Warriors vs WXM. Root cause, evidenced in that run's own logs: HLTV answered HTTP 403 to that runner for https://www.hltv.org/matches while every other source in the same run answered 200, and the identical read at 19:57Z the same evening answered 200. Sources.get treated that 403 as a permanent access restriction and blocked the host for the rest of the run, removing (a) the HLTV lineups scripts/vrs.py::identify() uses to choose between the two rosters Valve lists under one label and (b) the ranking-order research pick. Fixed in scripts/refresh.py: a rate-limited 403/429 is now waited out on the identical URL with identical headers (Retry-After honoured, then 10s/30s/60s, 120s cap) and only blocked if it keeps refusing; 401, 404 and region/challenge pages are unchanged. This is a pipeline-risk finding, not a coverage gap: the snapshot this site publishes (data/latest.json, generated 2026-10-16T19:59:25Z) reached HLTV with a 200 and carries a pick for all 21 open rows, so nothing published is affected. Recorded discrepancy — the board uses the earlier, conservative value, and this does not block publicationHotspawn describes its lock in two different places. The official how-it-works page says 'Your picks lock 5 minutes before the scheduled start time of the relevant match or game', while every CS2 row of its fixtures API read on 17 October 2026 at 00:50Z carries lock_offset_sec 360, i.e. six minutes before the scheduled start (the same value appears on every row of the page, so it is a feed-wide offset rather than a single odd row). This project derives the lock shown on each card from the API's own per-fixture lock_time, which is the earlier of the two by one minute and therefore the conservative choice: a pick is withheld at the API lock, never later. The discrepancy is published here rather than smoothed over, and it is not treated as a source failure.Hotspawn official rules: 'picks lock 5 minutes before the scheduled start time' ↗Hotspawn official fixture API (lock_offset_sec 360 on every CS2 row, read 17 October 00:50Z) ↗

Needs review — repository setting change required (owner action) (integrated into the arena/03befae8-maltaproj4 branch, session 03befae8)Two publishers write to the same Pages site and the site shows whichever deployed last. The repository's Pages source is still the legacy branch build (`main:/`, read from the Pages API on 2026-10-17), so every merge to `main` also triggers a legacy rebuild of the committed root fallback, while the verified workflow publishes the refreshed artifact. Measured on 2026-10-17: at ~00:33 UTC the live `build-info.json` was the verified artifact revision `9029e8f1abcc4459bc1efe73638b8b7fcfb19735`, built 00:15:28Z, 96 rows, snapshot 00:15:28Z, 29 of 34 open fixtures priced; at ~00:37 UTC the same URL served revision `b13968ebe6fa6f308c3accc60780640d553d43ba`, built 00:27:40Z, 83 rows, snapshot 16 Oct 19:59:25Z, 17 of 21 open fixtures priced — the root fallback that merge `0d77fe9` (PR #55) refreshed and the legacy build then published. Nothing is invented in either build and each is internally consistent; the defect is that the fallback can only ever hold the snapshot committed at staging time, so a merge can leave the public site on older data until the next verified publish. The freshness gate compares the live receipt with the commit being deployed (revision and a 60-minute snapshot budget) and re-publishes when either fails, and four scheduled slots an hour exist for exactly that, so the site converges again without intervention. This session tried the one-line fix — `PUT /repos/{owner}/{repo}/pages` with `build_type=workflow` — and the GitHub token available here returned 403 'Resource not accessible by integration'. Owner action, one step: repository Settings → Pages → Build and deployment → Source → GitHub Actions, after which the verified artifact is the only publisher and the root fallback stops being served. Until then the fallback stays in the repository, staged after build and validation, and every stale window is visible in the receipt above rather than hidden.Live build receipt (this URL is the proof: compare revision, rows and snapshot age) ↗Verified publisher workflow: build, validate, browser checks, deploy, live-byte verification ↗Run 38986175918 — artifact deploy for f8324205 with the live-byte verifier green ↗Legacy root-fallback stager (the file set the main:/ build republishes) ↗

Needs review — mitigation deployed; owner action still required (Pages source, see dual-publisher-race-2026-10-17)The published board froze on the 2026-10-18 build and withheld every pick for three days. Measured on 2026-10-21 at 21:20-21:35 UTC: the live build receipt served revision 88b1b2c3, built_at 2026-10-18T00:52:19Z with 98 rows, while the verified workflow had just built and deployed a 21:10:18Z snapshot from main; index.html and picks.html both rendered 'Snapshot expired or timestamp unverified. Current picks are withheld.' with every listed match marked 'Pick closed'. Cause, from the repository's own records: GitHub Pages is still configured build_type: legacy with source main:/ (GET /repos/.../pages, 2026-10-21T21:20Z; the PUT with build_type=workflow still answers HTTP 403 'Resource not accessible by integration'), so each push republishes the committed root fallback over the artifact. The last legacy build (commit 47c1b9d3, created 21:14:07Z, built 21:14:32Z) did exactly that, replacing the verified deployment 7237412459 (commit fcaf608b, 21:11:31Z). The committed root fallback itself was staged from the frozen 18 Oct bootstrap during PR #114 and nothing could refresh it: no stager ran on main, and scripts.stage_legacy had no rule against promoting an older or already-expired build. Mitigation now on main: scripts/publish_root_fallback is run by pages.yml on every main publication, stages the hash-verified public/ build (and its matching data/latest.json, so the committed fallback stays reproducible through scripts.validate --site .) only when the snapshot is inside the 8-hour evidence window, strictly newer than the committed one and older than a 4-hour cooldown, and then commits it with the workflow token. That keeps the legacy path serving the same bytes as the verified artifact and prevents a repeat of the backwards staging. The permanent fix remains a repository setting this integration cannot change: Settings → Pages → Build and deployment → Source → GitHub Actions.Live build receipt showing the 2026-10-18 build (98 rows) that the legacy source was serving ↗Public pick board during the freeze: 'Snapshot expired or timestamp unverified. Both retries are bounded, use the same URLs, never bypass a refusal and still fail closed afterwards; 401/403/429 handling is unchanged. Needs review — the register maps short and ambiguous labels globally; a per-fixture roster gate is recommendedTwo entries in the verified alias register map a label that is not the club's current competitive name to the current name, and the mapping applies to every fixture rather than to the one fixture it was verified on. (1) 'Zetta' and 'Alianza Lima Esports' are registered as one team, on the basis that the same five-player Peruvian squad played CCT South America 2026 Series #7 under the Alianza Lima tag; the board uses it to price Hotspawn fixture 1082837 (Zetta vs LOS NINOS, 2026-10-21T22:00Z) at 84.5% from Polymarket market 6001634, whose raw outcome labels are 'Alianza Lima Esports' and 'Los Niños'. (2) 'Betclic' alone is registered as 'Betclic Apogee Esports', verified once on the PGL Bucharest 2027 closed qualifier. Both mappings were correct for the fixture they were verified on, and both are narrow in intent, but scripts.core.norm applies them to every future fixture: a different squad playing under an Alianza Lima or Betclic tag, or a second Betclic-sponsored roster, would satisfy the name test. The project's identity policy says a rename is registered only with a dated primary source plus Valve's three-player roster-continuity rule; neither entry is re-checked against a lineup on the refresh that uses it. Recommendation: gate both aliases on the roster or the exact UTC start at pricing time, or restrict each alias to the fixture id recorded in its basis. Nothing is unpublished here: both rows' numbers re-derive from the retained raw prices, and this note records the risk rather than disputing the arithmetic. A third, separate tolerance route was found while auditing the 02:17Z board: fixture 1082882 is stored as 'UNiTY' / 'Apogee Betclic' but the market names 'UNiTY esports' / 'Betclic Apogee Esports'. Apogee Betclic is a register entry; 'UNiTY' is not, and the row matches only because Polymarket publishes the team abbreviation 'unity', which folds exactly to the fixture label. The methods page does license an operator's own published abbreviation, so the row is consistent with policy, but the route is a wider tolerance than a registered alias: abbreviations are short ('unity', 'fal', 'bca') and a future fixture whose name happens to equal some marketplace's abbreviation for a different club would match. Recommendation: record the abbreviation route explicitly on the row (fixture label, abbreviation, team id) and require the market's team id to be stable across observations, so a reader can see which tolerance priced the match.Hotspawn fixture 1082837 (Zetta vs LOS NINOS, 22:00Z 21 Oct 2026) — the row the Zetta alias prices ↗Polymarket market 6001634 raw outcomes 'Alianza Lima Esports' / 'Los Niños' ↗HLTV match 2399865 Zetta vs LOS NINOS (the identity anchor recorded for the Zetta alias) ↗HLTV match 2399654 Eternal Fire vs Betclic (the one fixture the short 'Betclic' label was verified on) ↗Polymarket market 5732890 'Eternal Fire vs Betclic Apogee Esports' ↗

Needs review — new originated-line source: Valve's VRS points as HLTV publishes them, used only where Valve's monthly file cannot resolve a clubSeven of the 35 open fixtures on the 22 Oct 00:55Z board carried a research-only pick with no percentage. Four of them failed the two-team line for one reason: Valve's GitHub standings file is published roughly monthly (newest file 2026-10-05 on this date), so a club that entered the ranking later has no row and the formula cannot run. Valve's ranking itself is continuous and HLTV publishes it dated at /valve-ranking/teams ('Valve global ranking on October 22nd, 2026'), with the same points value and roster per club (first row of that read: #1 Vitality, 1993 Valve points). The refresh now reads that page only for rows still unpriced after every market tier and the official-file model line, and reuses Valve's own formula (fit.js priorWinExpectation, RD 75, which reduces to the Glicko expected score), Valve's published calibration slope 0.7986, Valve's three-player roster-continuity rule and the same eight-hour evidence window. The row is published as model_vrs_mirror with the dated HLTV page linked, the official standings date it was needed instead of, and a 'not a market price' label. It fails closed: a page whose rows say 'HLTV points' rather than 'Valve points', a missing date header, fewer than 100 parsed rows, non-monotonic positions, a duplicated label with no lineup, or a club simply absent from the page each produce a recorded miss and no number. Limitation, stated on the row and here: the page is HLTV's rendering of Valve's ranking rather than Valve's own file, so it is second-hand provenance for the same official numbers; it is still a rating-based estimate, not a market line, and it cannot price a club that neither source ranks.HLTV's dated Valve global ranking read on 22 Oct 2026 (points and roster per club; #1 Vitality, 1993 Valve points) ↗Valve's own standings file used by the official-file line (dated 2026-10-05) ↗Valve's published expected-win formula (priorWinExpectation, RD 75) ↗Valve's published model-fit chart (slope 0.7986) ↗HLTV's regional Valve ranking view of the same date ↗

Needs review — the HLTV Valve-ranking mirror published live numbers for the first time, and a misleading probe marker is explainedUntil this run the mirror tier had never published a number: every scheduled board recorded 'HLTV Valve ranking mirror unavailable: HTTP 403', because the page was requested after ~50 HLTV page loads. The 2026-10-22 probe run measured the page itself answering 200 with 1.6 MB to this User-Agent, so the request was moved ahead of the per-fixture HLTV sweep and four rows (1079654, 1081149, 1081150, 1082926) now carry a percentage from valve-ranking/teams instead of a blank. The probe's raw-HTML marker search still reports 'Valve points x0', which is a property of the markup, not a failure: HLTV renders the unit inside a mobile-only span, '(1993<span class="gtSmartphone-only"> Valve</span> points)', so the literal substring never appears in the bytes while the page text reads '1993 Valve points'. The parser reads the rendered text of the points node, keys on the word Valve and refuses the world-ranking view, which says HLTV points. Limitation published on every mirror row: these points are Valve's ranking as HLTV publishes it, not Valve's monthly GitHub file, and the line is an originated model estimate, not a market price.HLTV dated view of Valve's global ranking (the mirror read) ↗Valve's own monthly standings file (the official source used when it resolves both clubs) ↗Probe run 39743668680 (page answers 200 / 1.6 MB; marker x0 on raw bytes) ↗

Repaired on main by session cecfe864/ea2e254c; this session adds the guard that makes a recurrence fail loudlyThe stylesheet the browser receives at the repository root (assets/style.css, which is what the legacy main:/ Pages source serves) is not regenerated by anything except a staged publish. When it drifted from the canonical site/assets/style.css by a single closing brace, the missing brace closed '@media(max-width:650px) and (max-height:700px)', so every later phone rule was nested inside a short-phone media query and did not apply on a normal phone. On the integrated tree the two copies are now byte-identical and balanced (verified: 728 opening and 728 closing braces in both). What this session adds is the guarantee that it cannot silently come back: scripts.stage_legacy.ensure_stylesheet_parity() repairs the root copy while staging, and scripts.validate.validate_stylesheet() fails any build whose served stylesheet is unbalanced or differs from the canonical copy, with unit tests covering both a missing and an extra brace. No rule, colour or layout value was changed by this session.Served root copy (assets/style.css) ↗Canonical stylesheet (site/assets/style.css) ↗Validator gate (scripts/validate.py) ↗Staging repair (scripts/stage_legacy.py) ↗

Policy change published 2026-10-24 (session acd2a4a8) — needs reviewAn exactly even (50/50) line at any tier — exchange, Pinnacle, researched book listing, or our VRS model/mirror — is no longer published as a pick-less probability. It is recorded on the row's attempts as rejected evidence and retained as neutral context (no_edge_lines), and the fallback ladder continues (HLTV, esportsbook lines, VRS model, HLTV research tiers), so the match can still receive a sourced pick instead of stranding the whole board (fixture 1079200, Color vs ex-Zero Tenacity, Polymarket market 6072812 at 0.5/0.5 with bid 0.49/ask 0.51 and no volume, blocked scheduled publish run 40097410704). Sessions ac5bcb06/2b856884 landed the same policy on main in parallel; this branch's harvest verified it end-to-end (20/20 picks on the 2026-10-24T17:00Z board). Limitation: a genuinely traded, liquid market that is truly even also falls through now; its 50/50 is retained in the rejected-quote/no-edge record but not displayed as a probability, and the pick (if any) comes from a lower tier and is labelled with that tier's method.Polymarket event: Color vs ex-Zero Tenacity (market 6072812) ↗Session acd2a4a8 harvest coverage summary ↗

Needs review — new publication guardscripts/quarantine.py now withholds any single open row that publishes a priced probability pair without a directional pick, or an exactly even pair. The row is published as a visible blank with its limitation and the rest of the board publishes normally; before this, one such row made every scheduled run refuse to commit the root, which is how the 09:03Z-16:00Z freeze happened. The rule deliberately lives in the publication path and not in verification-c6/audit_snapshot.py, because that auditor also replays archived historical snapshots, which must stay auditable under the rules of their own date. A correct row that could not be re-read failed scripts.validate, and that is the step that died. The repair is in scripts/validate.py: lineup_source_ok resolves the heading with a new same_club() that keeps the exact match as the primary rule and permits the identity register as the only bridge - both spellings inside one entry, that entry linking an https source, and the register keeping the fixture's two teams apart so one team's lineup can never satisfy the other. No fuzzy or substring matching was added, and no probability, roster overlap, pick orientation or freshness rule changed. Verified offline against the retained capture: scripts.validate now reproduces 78 rows, 17 calculations, 15 pages, 730 links, 20 checksums; the build reports 17 open rows, 17 with percentages, 0 unresolved; the full unit suite is 1,617 tests OK with the 11 new regressions in tests/test_session_b85ad9b4.py. For review: (1) the repair makes the validator accept an HLTV heading that differs from the Hotspawn label, and although that is gated on the sourced identity register, it is a widening of what counts as an HLTV roster source; (2) the same defect would have blocked any future snapshot where an originated model row belongs to a club whose HLTV heading differs from Hotspawn's spelling, so it was a standing trap, not a one-off; (3) nothing announces a red publisher publicly - the site's own expiry banner was the only signal, and that only appears after the eight-hour window has already lapsed.Scheduled publisher run whose 'Build and independently verify every row, calculation and internal link' step failed (2026-10-25 12:29Z) ↗Earlier scheduled run that failed on the same step (2026-10-25 06:56Z) ↗Last successful scheduled publication (2026-10-25 01:43Z), which stamped build-info.json 01:47:42Z ↗Non-publishing replay that identified the failing step and returned the assertion text ↗Return code of every publication step plus the failing output ↗HLTV fixture page whose heading for the club is 'Tropa do KinGui' (the alias this row needed) ↗Primary source recorded on the identity-register entry that links Projeto do KinGui, Tropa do KinGui and ex-Keyd Stars ↗Hotspawn CS2 pick'em lobby that lists fixture 1086697; Hotspawn supplies the fixture label, never a probability ↗Valve's official standings, the only inputs of the originated line on this row ↗The repaired offline re-check (same_club / lineup_source_ok) ↗The regressions, including the four ways the alias bridge must refuse ↗

Measured — the live site lagged the publish by ~26 minutes while every publish run was green; legacy Pages builds are soft-limited to 10 per hourWhile checking that its own merge reached visitors, session b06ee902 found the live deployment frozen at build-info built_at 2026-10-25T14:59:26Z while main's committed root already carried 15:00:47Z and later. The Pages build list explains it: 15:04:50 errored fd4ba08, 15:13:19 errored e379ee6, 15:20:18 errored 9cef2fb ('Page build failed.'), then 15:20:53 built f07cfc6, after which the live build-info advanced to 15:25:24Z with 75 rows. The site content was not the cause and was ruled out by diff: the last successful build before the burst (57d34f2, committed 15:02:17Z) and the first failing one (fd4ba08, 15:04:49Z) differ in exactly one file, COORDINATION.md, and the tree is 120 MB across 2,561 files with no symlinks, so neither the 1 GB site limit nor the 100 MB per-file limit is in play. GitHub documents the actual constraint: 'GitHub Pages sites have a soft limit of 10 builds per hour. This limit does not apply if you build and publish your site with a custom GitHub Actions workflow.' This repository is published with build_type legacy and main receives commits every few minutes from many concurrent sessions, so builds exceed that soft limit and the served site stays on the last successful deployment until a slot frees. Nothing is broken and no pick is wrong: the board is simply older than the repository during a burst, and a visitor reads an older snapshot than the run that just succeeded. The remedy (switching Pages to a GitHub Actions deployment) is a repository-settings change outside this session's file scope, so it is recorded here for the owner rather than applied unilaterally while other sessions publish through the legacy main:/ root fallback.GitHub Pages limits — 10 builds per hour soft limit, exempt for custom Actions workflows ↗Pages build list showing the errored/built sequence ↗Live build that the deployment advanced to ↗Session receipt with the same measurement ↗
